Tool result:
/**
 * The first-run walkthrough for a Read, Recall, Colour section.
 *
 * A student's first section is the only one she does not already know the
 * shape of, and the briefing page she read a minute ago is gone by the time
 * the first stimulus is on screen. So for the first three stimuli only, a
 * quiet card sits beside the paper and says what to do now and what happens
 * next. After that it goes, and it never comes back.
 *
 * Two hard rules, both about not getting in the way:
 *
 * - **It is never in the loop.** It takes no key, blocks nothing, judges
 *   nothing and gates nothing. Space still does the same thing it always did,
 *   at the same moment. The card is text next to the drill, not a step in it.
 * - **It ends by itself.** Three stimuli, then it is gone whether or not she
 *   dismissed it, and the "seen" flag is written the first time a section
 *   starts, so a second section is bare even if the first was abandoned.
 */

/** How many stimuli the walkthrough covers before it retires itself. */
export const COACHED_STIMULI = 3;

const KEY = "rrc.section.walkthrough.seen";

function storageKey(studentId: string): string {
  return `${KEY}.${studentId}`;
}

/** Has this student already been walked through a section? */
export function hasSeenWalkthrough(studentId: string): boolean {
  try {
    return window.localStorage.getItem(storageKey(studentId)) === "1";
  } catch {
    // A browser refusing storage should not force the walkthrough on every
    // run for ever; treating it as seen is the less annoying failure.
    return true;
  }
}

/** Remember that it has been shown. Called once, when the first run starts. */
export function markWalkthroughSeen(studentId: string): void {
  try {
    window.localStorage.setItem(storageKey(studentId), "1");
  } catch {
    /* storage refused; nothing to do */
  }
}

export interface CoachNote {
  /** 1-based, of `COACHED_STIMULI`. */
  step: number;
  of: number;
  title: string;
  body: string;
  /** What happens the moment she presses Space. */
  next: string;
}

const WORKING: { title: string; body: string; next: string }[] = [
  {
    title: "Read it, out loud in pieces",
    body: "Take the stimulus a piece at a time. After each piece, say it back in your own words, then say what that piece is doing in the argument — the colour. Evidence, conclusion, someone else's view, a concession. Keep talking; nothing here is being marked as you go.",
    next: "Space hides the text and asks you to recall the whole thing.",
  },
  {
    title: "Same again, and do not vet yourself",
    body: "Second one. Say the wrong word rather than pausing to find the right one — the drill is training you to keep reading and talking at once, so a rough colour said out loud beats a perfect one thought silently.",
    next: "Space hides the text for the recall.",
  },
  {
    title: "Last one with help",
    body: "You have the rhythm now: read a piece, say it back, colour it. From the next stimulus on, this card is gone and it is just you, the paper and the clock.",
    next: "Space hides the text for the recall.",
  },
];

const RECALLING: { title: string; body: string; next: string }[] = [
  {
    title: "Now say it back from memory",
    body: "The text is gone on purpose. Recap the whole argument out loud — what was claimed, what it rested on, where it turned. It does not need to be word perfect; it needs to be the argument.",
    next: "Space brings up the next stimulus.",
  },
  {
    title: "Recall it whole",
    body: "Again from memory, whole argument. If you lose a piece, say so out loud and carry on — a gap you name is worth more than a silence.",
    next: "Space brings up the next stimulus.",
  },
  {
    title: "Recall it, then you are on your own",
    body: "Say the argument back one more time. After this the card retires and the section runs straight through to the end, one take, no stopping.",
    next: "Space brings up the next stimulus.",
  },
];

/**
 * The note for where she is, or null once the walkthrough is over.
 *
 * `item` is 0-based, matching the cursor.
 */
export function coachNote(item: number, phase: "working" | "recalling"): CoachNote | null {
  if (item < 0 || item >= COACHED_STIMULI) return null;
  const source = phase === "working" ? WORKING : RECALLING;
  const note = source[item];
  if (!note) return null;
  return { step: item + 1, of: COACHED_STIMULI, ...note };
}
=====STAGE
import type { SectionPhase, SectionStimulus } from "./section";
import type { CoachNote } from "./coach";
import { VoiceLevel } from "./VoiceLevel";
import { useNoKeyboard } from "./noKeyboard";

/**
 * Is this page running inside another page's frame?
 *
 * A preview frame is never handed the microphone: the embed carries no
 * microphone permission, so `getUserMedia` is refused before the student is
 * ever asked. That looks identical to a student saying no, and "allow the
 * microphone for this site" is advice she cannot act on. So when the drill is
 * framed, the refusal names the real cause and offers the one thing that
 * works: the same address in a tab of its own.
 *
 * A cross-origin frame throws on the read, and a throw means framed.
 */
function isEmbedded(): boolean {
  try {
    return window.self !== window.top;
  } catch {
    return true;
  }
}


/**
 * One screen of a Read, Recall, Colour SECTION run.
 *
 * Presentational, like `RecallStage`, and separate from it because the rule it
 * keeps is different. v1 hides one chunk during its recall; a section has two
 * looks and nothing between them: the whole stimulus while she works it, and
 * no stimulus at all during The Recall
 * (`docs/adr/0013-she-chunks-freely-and-the-app-does-not-track-it.md`).
 *
 * **It wears the real section's clothes.** The drill she is training for is sat
 * on LawHub, and `OfficialDrillFrame` already mirrors that surface: indigo
 * field, white header rail, one centred white paper card, the stimulus in
 * Fraunces at 19px. This screen replicates that contract exactly and removes
 * the answering half -- no question column, no choices, no verdict, no
 * notepad -- so the drill feels like the thing it is preparing her for rather
 * than like a separate app. The frame itself is not imported because it carries
 * auth, the notepad and the tutor toggle, none of which may exist inside this
 * loop, and because this component has to stay presentational.
 *
 * The hard requirements, all of them negative:
 *
 * - **No place marker.** The app does not track which chunk she is on, so no
 *   chunk is styled as current, done, or upcoming. She reads, recalls and
 *   colours in whatever units she likes.
 * - **The text truly vanishes for The Recall.** Not blurred, not transparent,
 *   not aria-hidden: removed. A stimulus left in the DOM would be read back to
 *   a student using a screen reader, which defeats the drill for her exactly.
 * - **One key.** No mouse in the loop. The only control here is Space, wired
 *   by the runner; the exit is outside the loop.
 * - **No answer choices, and nothing judged.** Nothing is scored, shown or
 *   corrected until the section ends, because a live verdict trains her to vet
 *   before speaking, which is the habit colouring exists to break.
 *
 * The clock counts up rather than down, and the microphone has one quiet
 * indicator beside the instruction. Both are late additions and both are
 * concessions to the same finding: a student cannot pace a 25-minute section
 * she cannot see the time on, and she cannot tell a dead microphone from her
 * own silence. Neither may ever read as a score.
 */

export interface SectionStageProps {
  stimulus: SectionStimulus;
  phase: SectionPhase;
  /** 1-based, for the quiet position line. */
  stimulusNumber: number;
  totalStimuli: number;
  /**
   * A microphone fault, when there is one. Silence and a dead input look
   * identical from her side, so saying nothing while she talks into nothing is
   * the worst version of this.
   */
  error?: string | null;
  /** Leaving mid-run. A full-screen activity with no way out is a trap. */
  onExit?: () => void;
  /** Seconds since the section started. Omit to draw no clock at all. */
  elapsedSeconds?: number;
  /**
   * True while the microphone is open and the file is being written. Drawn as
   * a plain "Recording" mark in the rail so she never has to wonder, mid-run,
   * whether the thing is on. The voice bar in the footer answers "is anything
   * reaching it"; this answers the prior question.
   */
  recording?: boolean;
  /**
   * The live input, for the presence indicator. Omit and no indicator is drawn,
   * which is what a run against a handheld recorder looks like.
   */
  getStream?: () => MediaStream | null;
  /**
   * Whether nothing is reaching the microphone. Raised at a boundary once the
   * silence has run past one window, and withdrawn as soon as anything is heard.
   */
  onDeadInput?: (dead: boolean) => void;
  /**
   * The microphone was refused or is missing. The ready beat holds here rather
   * than counting down into a spoken run that has nothing to mark at the end.
   */
  micRefused?: boolean;
  /** Ask for the microphone again, from the beat, before anything has started. */
  onRetryMic?: () => void;
  /**
   * Leaving is confirmed rather than done, because a half-finished section
   * writes nothing by design and the button is on screen for the whole run.
   */
  confirmingExit?: boolean;
  onConfirmExit?: () => void;
  onCancelExit?: () => void;
  /**
   * Seconds left in the ready beat, or null once the section is running.
   *
   * The recorder is already open during it, on purpose: the beat exists so the
   * first thing an unrepeatable take captures is not a student still settling,
   * and so she can watch the level move before any of it counts.
   */
  countdown?: number | null;
  /**
   * Advance, for a device with no Space key to press.
   *
   * The same `press()` the keyboard calls, threaded through because this
   * component is presentational. It is rendered only where there is no
   * keyboard (`noKeyboard.ts`), so the desktop loop stays one key with no mouse
   * in it, and Space stays authoritative on every device including this one.
   *
   * Without it, "warn the phone and let her through" is a trap: she can read
   * stimulus 1 and never reach stimulus 2.
   */
  onAdvance?: () => void;
  /**
   * The first-run walkthrough note, for the first few stimuli only.
   *
   * Text beside the paper, never a step in the loop: it takes no key, gates
   * nothing and is gone after the third stimulus whether or not she dismissed
   * it. Omit and no card is drawn at all, which is every run after the first.
   */
  coach?: CoachNote | null;
  onDismissCoach?: () => void;
  /**
   * She has stepped away. The clock is stopped and nothing is being recorded.
   *
   * The stimulus comes OFF the screen for the whole of it, and that is the
   * feature rather than a side effect of it: a paused stimulus she can still
   * read is not a break, it is untimed reading time on a timed drill, and it
   * would hollow the section out without anybody deciding to.
   */
  paused?: boolean;
  /** Step away. Omitted before the section starts, where there is nothing to pause. */
  onPause?: () => void;
  onResume?: () => void;
  /**
   * A pause the browser would not give her, or a recording that did not come
   * back. Said on the paused card, because that is where she is standing.
   */
  pauseError?: string | null;
}

/*
 * The working instruction covers steps 1 to 3 at once, because the screen no
 * longer walks her through them: she reads, says back and colours in whatever
 * units she likes, and the app is not told where she is. The Recall's
 * instruction is the method's own: the text is gone, the whole argument comes
 * back from memory.
 */
const INSTRUCTION: Record<SectionPhase, string> = {
  working: "Read it. Tell it back and colour it in as you go.",
  recalling: "Say the whole argument back from memory.",
};

/** What the one key does next, which is the whole of the controls. */
const KEY_DOES: Record<SectionPhase, string> = {
  working: "recall full stimulus",
  recalling: "next stimulus",
};

/** mm:ss, and hh:mm:ss only if a section ever ran past an hour. */
function clock(totalSeconds: number): string {
  const s = Math.max(0, Math.floor(totalSeconds));
  const mm = Math.floor(s / 60);
  const ss = String(s % 60).padStart(2, "0");
  return `${mm}:${ss}`;
}

export function SectionStage({
  stimulus,
  phase,
  stimulusNumber,
  totalStimuli,
  error,
  onExit,
  elapsedSeconds,
  recording = false,
  getStream,
  onDeadInput,
  micRefused,
  onRetryMic,
  confirmingExit,
  onConfirmExit,
  onCancelExit,
  countdown = null,
  onAdvance,
  coach = null,
  onDismissCoach,
  paused = false,
  onPause,
  onResume,
  pauseError = null,
}: SectionStageProps) {
  const noKeyboard = useNoKeyboard();
  // Position across the section, drawn as the rail's fill. Progress inside the
  // current stimulus is deliberately not shown: the app does not know it.
  const through = Math.min(stimulusNumber - 1, totalStimuli) / Math.max(totalStimuli, 1);

  return (
    <div className="ac-section">
      {/* The white rail, as on the real section: chrome above the paper, never
          on it. It carries where she is and the way out, and nothing else. */}
      <header className="ac-section-head">
        <span className="ac-section-where">
          Stimulus <b>{stimulusNumber}</b> of {totalStimuli}
        </span>
        <div className="ac-section-rail" aria-hidden>
          <span className="ac-section-rail-fill" style={{ transform: `scaleX(${through})` }} />
        </div>
        {/*
          * Elapsed, counting UP, against the goal.
          *
          * This screen carried no clock at all, on the reasoning that a running
          * clock produces the rushed reading the drill exists to treat. The
          * drill is against the clock, though, and a student with no way to see
          * the time cannot pace a 25-minute section. Counting up rather than
          * down is the compromise: she can see where she is without a number
          * falling towards zero in the corner of her eye.
          */}
        {elapsedSeconds !== undefined && (
          <span className="ac-section-clock" aria-live="off">
            {clock(elapsedSeconds)}
          </span>
        )}
        {/* Said in words as well as drawn, so it survives grayscale and a
            screen reader alike. Announced once when it turns on; never
            re-announced. */}
        {recording ? (
          <span className="ac-section-rec" role="status">
            <i aria-hidden="true" />
            Recording
          </span>
        ) : paused ? (
          <span className="ac-section-rec ac-section-rec--paused" role="status">
            <i aria-hidden="true" />
            Paused
          </span>
        ) : (
          <span className="ac-section-rec ac-section-rec--off" aria-hidden="true" />
        )}
        {/* Beside the way out rather than near the paper: it is chrome, it is
            not a step in the drill, and it must never sit where a hand aiming
            for Space could find it. Gone once she is paused -- the card behind
            it is the only thing that speaks then. */}
        {onPause && !paused ? (
          <button type="button" className="ac-section-pause" onClick={onPause}>
            Pause
          </button>
        ) : (
          <span />
        )}
        {onExit ? (
          <button type="button" className="ac-section-exit" onClick={onExit}>
            Leave
          </button>
        ) : (
          <span />
        )}
      </header>

      {/*
        * Leaving throws the run away, so it says so first.
        *
        * A half-finished section is deliberately not a partial score and never
        * becomes one, which is the right rule and is what makes one unguarded
        * click on a permanently visible button cost up to twenty-five minutes.
        * The confirmation is the whole guard: the wording names what goes, and
        * staying is the default the keyboard lands on.
        */}
      {confirmingExit ? (
        <div className="ac-section-confirm" role="dialog" aria-modal="true" aria-label="Leave this section">
          <div className="ac-section-confirm-card">
            <p className="ac-section-confirm-title">Leave this section?</p>
            <p className="ac-section-confirm-body">
              A section is scored whole. Stopping now throws away{" "}
              {elapsedSeconds !== undefined ? clock(elapsedSeconds) : "everything"} of work and the
              recording with it. Nothing is kept and there is no partial score.
            </p>
            <div className="ac-section-confirm-row">
              <button
                type="button"
                className="ac-section-confirm-stay"
                onClick={onCancelExit}
                autoFocus
              >
                Keep going
              </button>
              <button type="button" className="ac-section-confirm-go" onClick={onConfirmExit}>
                Leave and lose it
              </button>
            </div>
          </div>
        </div>
      ) : null}

      <main className="ac-section-field">
        <div className="ac-section-paper">
          {paused ? (
            /*
             * The break, and the whole of what she can see during it.
             *
             * The stimulus is not behind this, blurred or dimmed or scrolled
             * off -- it is not rendered, for the same reason it is not rendered
             * during The Recall: a text left in the DOM is read aloud to a
             * student on a screen reader, and a text she can reach at all turns
             * a stopped clock into free reading time. The one thing on the card
             * is the way back.
             */
            <div className="ac-section-ready ac-section-ready--paused">
              <p className="ac-section-ready-title">Paused</p>
              <p className="ac-section-ready-body">
                The clock is stopped and nothing is being recorded. Your work so far is safe and
                your time is not running. The stimulus comes back when you do.
              </p>
              {pauseError ? (
                <p className="ac-section-error" role="status">
                  {pauseError}
                </p>
              ) : null}
              <div className="ac-section-ready-actions">
                {/* The keyboard lands here, so the key already under her thumb
                    is the one that starts the section again -- and because it
                    is a button, the runner's own Space handler stands down and
                    cannot advance the drill from behind this card. */}
                <button
                  type="button"
                  className="ac-section-ready-retry"
                  onClick={onResume}
                  autoFocus
                >
                  Back to it
                </button>
                {onExit && (
                  <button type="button" className="ac-section-ready-leave" onClick={onExit}>
                    Leave this section
                  </button>
                )}
              </div>
            </div>
          ) : countdown !== null ? (
            /*
             * The beat before the first stimulus.
             *
             * The stimulus is deliberately not behind this: it has not started
             * yet, and a section that opened with its first argument already
             * readable would be a section she had begun reading before the
             * recording was worth anything.
             */
            <div className="ac-section-ready" data-mic={micRefused ? "refused" : "live"}>
              {micRefused ? (
                /*
                 * The heading is the fault, not a claim the screen cannot back.
                 *
                 * It said "Recording now" while the footer said nothing was
                 * being recorded, and then counted down into twenty-five
                 * minutes of spoken work that the grader refuses for having no
                 * recording. The consequence is the thing she needs, so the
                 * consequence is what it says, and the two ways forward are
                 * both here rather than neither.
                 */
                <>
                  <p className="ac-section-ready-title">No microphone.</p>
                  <p className="ac-section-ready-body">
                    This drill is spoken all the way through, so without one there is nothing to
                    mark at the end. Allow the microphone for this site in your browser, then ask
                    again. Nothing has started, and your time has not begun.
                  </p>
                  {isEmbedded() && (
                    <p className="ac-section-ready-body">
                      This page is inside a preview window, and a preview window is never given the
                      microphone. Open the drill in its own tab and start it there.
                    </p>
                  )}
                  <div className="ac-section-ready-actions">
                    <button type="button" className="ac-section-ready-retry" onClick={onRetryMic}>
                      Ask again
                    </button>
                    {isEmbedded() && (
                      <button
                        type="button"
                        className="ac-section-ready-retry"
                        onClick={() =>
                          window.open(window.location.href, "_blank", "noopener,noreferrer")
                        }
                      >
                        Open in its own tab
                      </button>
                    )}
                    {onExit && (
                      <button type="button" className="ac-section-ready-leave" onClick={onExit}>
                        Leave this section
                      </button>
                    )}
                  </div>
                </>

              ) : (
                <>
                  <p className="ac-section-ready-title">Recording now</p>
                  <p className="ac-section-ready-body">
                    One take, all the way through, out loud. Space twice per stimulus: once when
                    you have read it and said it back, once when you have given your Read.
                  </p>
                  <span className="ac-section-ready-count">
                    {countdown > 0 ? `Starting in ${countdown}` : "Starting"}
                  </span>
                </>
              )}
            </div>
          ) : (
            <div className="ac-section-body">
              {/*
                While she works, every chunk is rendered in order with nothing
                marking one out from another: the cuts guide her eye and hold the
                grader's Details, and that is all they do. During The Recall the
                stimulus is not rendered at all -- the paper stays, the text goes,
                and it does not come back until the Report Card.
              */}
              {phase === "working" ? (
                <div className="ac-section-stimulus">
                  {stimulus.chunks.map((chunk) => (
                    <span key={chunk.n} className="ac-section-chunk">
                      {chunk.text}{" "}
                    </span>
                  ))}
                </div>
              ) : null}
            </div>
          )}

          {/* The card's own footer, where the real section keeps its controls.
              What she is doing sits left, the one key that does it sits right. */}
          <div className="ac-section-foot">
            <p className="ac-section-instruction" role="status">
              {paused
                ? "Paused. Nothing is being recorded."
                : countdown !== null
                  ? micRefused
                    ? "The section has not started."
                    : "Say something to check your microphone."
                  : INSTRUCTION[phase]}
              {/* No presence bar while she is away. The microphone is still
                  open -- pausing a recorder does not close the input -- so a
                  bar left running would move to an empty room and say the one
                  thing it exists to say when it moves: that this is being
                  heard. */}
              {getStream && !paused ? (
                <VoiceLevel getStream={getStream} phase={phase} onDeadInput={onDeadInput} />
              ) : null}
            </p>
            {/* The fault and the instruction, not the fault INSTEAD of it.
                These were a ternary, so a microphone warning took away the only
                thing on the screen telling her which key advances the drill --
                on precisely the run where something has already gone wrong. The
                warning leads, because it is the new information. */}
            <div className="ac-section-foot-right">
              {/* A pause she asked for and did not get.
                  It belongs in the footer rather than on the paused card
                  precisely BECAUSE she is not paused: the card she would have
                  read it on is the one that never appeared, and a refusal with
                  nowhere to be said is a button that does nothing. */}
              {!paused && pauseError ? (
                <p className="ac-section-error" role="status">
                  {pauseError}
                </p>
              ) : null}
              {error ? (
                <p className="ac-section-error" role="status">
                  {error}
                </p>
              ) : null}
              <p className="ac-section-hint">
                <kbd>space</kbd>{" "}
                {paused
                  ? "back to it"
                  : countdown !== null
                    ? micRefused
                      ? "once the microphone is on"
                      : "in a moment"
                    : KEY_DOES[phase]}
              </p>
              {/* The same act as Space, for a device that has no Space.
                  Rendered only where there is no keyboard, so the desktop loop
                  is untouched and no mouse enters it. Disabled through the beat
                  for the same reason the hint reads "in a moment": nothing may
                  advance before the section has started. */}
              {noKeyboard && onAdvance ? (
                <button
                  type="button"
                  className="ac-section-advance"
                  onClick={onAdvance}
                  disabled={countdown !== null || paused}
                >
                  {countdown !== null
                    ? micRefused
                      ? "Waiting for the microphone"
                      : "Starting"
                    : KEY_DOES[phase] === "recall full stimulus"
                      ? "Recall full stimulus"
                      : "Next stimulus"}
                </button>
              ) : null}
            </div>
          </div>
        </div>

        {/* The first-run walkthrough. Beside the paper, never on it, and it
            holds nothing up: no key of its own, nothing to acknowledge, gone
            of its own accord after the third stimulus. */}
        {coach && countdown === null && !paused ? (
          <aside className="ac-section-coach" aria-label="Walkthrough">
            <p className="ac-section-coach-step">
              Walkthrough · {coach.step} of {coach.of}
            </p>
            <p className="ac-section-coach-title">{coach.title}</p>
            <p className="ac-section-coach-body">{coach.body}</p>
            <p className="ac-section-coach-next">
              <span>Next</span> {coach.next}
            </p>
            {onDismissCoach ? (
              <button type="button" className="ac-section-coach-hide" onClick={onDismissCoach}>
                Hide this
              </button>
            ) : null}
          </aside>
        ) : null}
      </main>
    </div>
  );
}

import { useCallback, useEffect, useMemo, useRef, useState } from "react";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
import type { Database } from "@/integrations/supabase/types";
import { completeSectionRun, requestSectionGrading } from "../api/activitiesClient";
import { SectionStage } from "./SectionStage";
import { coachNote, hasSeenWalkthrough, markWalkthroughSeen } from "./coach";
import { useSectionRecorder, type RecordingSegment } from "./useSectionRecorder";

import { useEffect, useRef } from "react";
import type { SectionPhase } from "./section";

/**
 * One quiet element that moves with her voice, and the only evidence on screen
 * that the microphone is alive.
 *
 * For twenty-one minutes she talks into a blank card. The recorder already
 * reports a REFUSED microphone, but it cannot see a live-but-silent one -- a
 * muted input, a device the OS switched underneath her, a headset that dropped
 * off. That failure produces a perfect-looking run, a clean upload, and a
 * Report Card of zeros, and she finds out at the end of the twenty-one minutes.
 *
 * So this does two jobs with one reading.
 *
 * **Presence.** A bar that answers "is anything reaching it" without answering
 * "how am I doing". Deliberately not a level meter with a target: nothing here
 * may look like a score, because nothing is judged until the section ends.
 *
 * **The fault.** If the input never leaves the noise floor across a whole
 * stimulus, that is said out loud at the boundary, while there is still a
 * section left to save -- and unsaid the moment anything is heard again.
 *
 * Nothing here re-renders the runner. The bar is written to as a DOM node
 * inside an animation frame, because a component that set state at 60Hz would
 * be re-rendering the drill room underneath her while she reads.
 */

/**
 * The line between "an open microphone in a quiet room" and "nothing arriving".
 *
 * RMS over the raw time-domain samples, so it is amplitude and not loudness. A
 * genuinely dead input sits at or near zero; a live one in a silent room still
 * carries the room. Set low on purpose: this exists to catch nothing at all,
 * never to judge a quiet speaker, and a false "your microphone is dead" during
 * a real run is worse than the fault it reports.
 */
const NOISE_FLOOR = 0.004;

import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from "react";
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";
import { Button } from "@/components/ui/button";
import {
  getSectionGrade,
  requestSectionGrading,
  startSectionFocusAttempt,
} from "../api/activitiesClient";
import type { SectionGrade } from "../api/types";
import { SectionReportCard } from "./SectionReportCard";

/**
 * The wait between finishing a section and seeing the Report Card.
 *
 * Grading is a post-section job: one transcription of ~25 minutes of audio plus
 * one judgement call per stimulus, run server-side after she stops. It does not
 * finish inside the request that ends the section, and it must not be tied to
 * this tab staying open.
 *
 * So this polls for the grade rather than waiting on a response. The ABSENCE of
 * a row is the pending signal (ADR 0019): there is no status column to trust,
 * and none she could set herself. If it never arrives, the honest thing to show
 * is that it is still being marked, not an error and certainly not a zero. The
 * sweeper picks up anything this tab missed.
 */

const POLL_MS = 4000;
/** After this, stop asking and tell her it is still being marked. */
const GIVE_UP_MS = 3 * 60 * 1000;
/**
 * How long to wait before asking for the grade a second time.
 *
 * The runner already asked once, fire and forget, and that ask can be lost: the
 * tab closed before it left, the network dropped, the function was cold. One
 * repeat after a decent wait recovers all three.
 *
 * The wait has to be longer than a grading actually takes. At 20 seconds the
 * repeat arrived while the first grading was still transcribing, nothing was on
 * record yet, and both ran to the end: two transcriptions, two sets of verdicts
 * on the same audio, and a card that showed the first while the database kept
 * the second. A real section grades in one to three minutes. The grader now
 * also refuses to start while another grading of the same Attempt is running,
 * so a repeat that does land early costs one request rather than a second
 * grading; this wait is what keeps that from being the ordinary path.
 */
const RETRY_ASK_MS = 120000;

interface SectionReportProps {
  assignmentId: string;
  onDone?: () => void;
}

export function SectionReport({ assignmentId, onDone }: SectionReportProps) {
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const [grade, setGrade] = useState<SectionGrade | null>(null);
  const [stillWaiting, setStillWaiting] = useState(false);
  const startedAt = useRef(Date.now());

  useEffect(() => {
    let cancelled = false;
    let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>;
    let askedAgain = false;

    const poll = async () => {
      try {
        const found = await getSectionGrade(assignmentId);
        if (cancelled) return;
        if (found) {
          setGrade(found);
          return;
        }
      } catch {
        // A failed read is not a failed run. Keep waiting; the row either
        // arrives or the give-up copy takes over.
      }
      if (cancelled) return;

      const waited = Date.now() - startedAt.current;
      if (!askedAgain && waited > RETRY_ASK_MS) {
        // Ask once more. Losing the first ask is the likeliest way a finished
        // run ends up with no Report Card, and it is the one failure this
        // screen is in a position to repair by itself.
        askedAgain = true;
        void requestSectionGrading(assignmentId);
      }
      if (waited > GIVE_UP_MS) {
        setStillWaiting(true);
        return;
      }
      timer = setTimeout(poll, POLL_MS);
    };

    void poll();
    return () => {
      cancelled = true;
      clearTimeout(timer);
    };
  }, [assignmentId]);

  if (grade)
    return (
      <SectionReportCard
        grade={grade}
        onDone={onDone}
        onStartFocusSet={async (slugs) => {
          // A fresh Attempt built server-side from this run's weakest stimuli,
          // entered straight into the runner the way the library enters it.
          const id = await startSectionFocusAttempt(assignmentId, slugs);
          navigate(`/activities/${id}`, { state: { startSection: true } });
        }}
      />
    );

  return (
    <div className="mx-auto max-w-2xl">
      <div className="rounded-[28px] border border-border bg-[hsl(var(--ac-surface))] p-8 text-center shadow-[0_2px_24px_rgba(30,24,16,0.06)] md:p-12">
        <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.2em] text-muted-foreground">
          Section finished
        </p>
        <p className="ac-display mt-3 text-4xl text-foreground">
          {stillWaiting ? "Still being marked" : "Marking your run"}
        </p>
        <p className="mt-4 text-sm text-muted-foreground">
          {stillWaiting
            ? "This one is taking longer than usual. Your run is saved and your Report Card will be here when you come back to it."
            : "Your recording is being read back against every detail. This takes a minute."}
        </p>
        {onDone && (
          <div className="mt-8">
            <Button variant={stillWaiting ? "default" : "outline"} onClick={onDone}>
              Done
            </Button>
          </div>
        )}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}
import { useMemo } from "react";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";
import { bandFor } from "../recall/grading";
import { tallyMisses } from "../recall/misses";
import { SEED_ACTIVITIES } from "../content";
import type { SectionGrade, SectionGradeStub } from "../api/types";

/**
 * Every section run on record, and what keeps slipping across all of them.
 *
 * The drill's own instruction is three a day, six if she can. Nothing in the
 * product knew she had done four: a Report Card is one run, and one run cannot
 * tell her whether she is getting better. Twenty runs can, and they were all
 * already stored.
 *
 * The same component answers both questions worth asking of a pile of runs, and
 * they are different questions:
 *
 * - **Is this working?** Coverage over time, run by run, next to the clock.
 * - **What do I keep dropping?** Not which Details, which KIND of Detail, as a
 *   share of the times that kind came up. "You dropped 'all employees'" is a
 *   fact about a stimulus; "you drop the quantifier two times in three" is a
 *   fact about her, and only the second one changes what she practises.
 *
 * Read-only and derived. Nothing here is stored, so a better grouping is a
 * deploy rather than a backfill, and this cannot disagree with the Report Cards
 * it is built from.
 */

/**
 * A run, in either of the two shapes the app reads them in.
 *
 * The table needs only the summary, which is the light read the section library
 * makes for every run she has ever done. "What keeps slipping" needs the
 * per-stimulus misses, which is the heavier read, so it is made for the recent
 * few and the rest arrive without it. A run with no `stimuli` contributes to the
 * table and not to the tally, rather than being left out of both.
 */
type Run = SectionGradeStub & Partial<Pick<SectionGrade, "stimuli">>;

interface SectionRunHistoryProps {
  grades: Run[];
  /** Whose runs these are, when the reader is not the student. */
  heading?: string;
  emptyMessage?: string;
}

const BAND_TEXT: Record<ReturnType<typeof bandFor>, string> = {
  "at-the-standard": "text-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))]",
  "nearly-there": "text-[hsl(38_92%_34%)]",
  thin: "text-[hsl(24_85%_42%)]",
  "lost-it": "text-[hsl(350_80%_45%)]",
};

function minutes(seconds: number): string {
  const m = Math.floor(seconds / 60);
  return `${m}:${String(Math.round(seconds % 60)).padStart(2, "0")}`;
}

/**
 * Every Detail each stimulus asks for, from the content already in the bundle.
 *
 * The grade stores the Details she MISSED and a count of the ones she carried,
 * not their text, so a share of what was asked cannot be computed from grades
 * alone. It does not have to be: the sections are authored content and ship
 * with the app, so the denominator is a lookup rather than a query.
 */
const DETAILS_BY_SLUG: Map<string, string[]> = new Map(
  SEED_ACTIVITIES.filter((a) => a.mode === "section").flatMap((a) =>
    (a.items as unknown as { slug: string; chunks: { details: { text: string }[] }[] }[]).map(
      (item) => [item.slug, item.chunks.flatMap((c) => c.details.map((d) => d.text))] as const,
    ),
  ),
);

export function SectionRunHistory({
  grades,
  heading = "Read, Recall, Colour",
  emptyMessage = "No sections run yet.",
}: SectionRunHistoryProps) {
  const runs = useMemo(
    () => [...grades].sort((a, b) => b.graded_at.localeCompare(a.graded_at)),
    [grades],
  );

  const slipping = useMemo(
    () =>
      tallyMisses(
        runs.flatMap((g) =>
          (g.stimuli ?? [])
            // A stimulus the judge never answered for has an empty `missed` and
            // is not evidence of anything. Counting it would report our own
            // outage as her carrying every Detail in it.
            .filter((s) => !s.ungraded)
            .map((s) => ({ missed: s.missed, asked: DETAILS_BY_SLUG.get(s.slug) ?? [] })),
        ),
      ),
    [runs],
  );

  if (runs.length === 0) {
    return <p className="text-sm text-muted-foreground">{emptyMessage}</p>;
  }

  return (
    <div className="space-y-6">
      <div>
        <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.16em] text-muted-foreground">
          {heading}
        </p>
        <p className="mt-1 text-sm text-foreground">
          {runs.length} {runs.length === 1 ? "section" : "sections"} run, most recent first
        </p>
      </div>

      <div className="overflow-x-auto">
        <table className="w-full min-w-[34rem] text-sm">
          <thead>
            <tr className="border-b border-border text-left text-xs uppercase tracking-[0.12em] text-muted-foreground">
              <th className="py-2 pr-4 font-medium">When</th>
              <th className="py-2 pr-4 font-medium">Section</th>
              <th className="py-2 pr-4 font-medium">Held</th>
              <th className="py-2 pr-4 font-medium">Understood</th>
              <th className="py-2 font-medium">Time</th>
            </tr>
          </thead>
          <tbody>
            {runs.map((g) => {
              const band = bandFor(g.section.mean_coverage);
              return (
                <tr key={`${g.assignment_id}-${g.attempt_no}`} className="border-b border-border/60">
                  <td className="py-2 pr-4 text-muted-foreground">
                    {new Date(g.graded_at).toLocaleDateString(undefined, {
                      day: "numeric",
                      month: "short",
                    })}
                  </td>
                  <td className="py-2 pr-4 text-foreground">
                    {g.source_section}
                    {g.attempt_no > 1 ? (
                      <span className="text-muted-foreground"> · {g.attempt_no}</span>
                    ) : null}
                  </td>
                  <td className={cn("py-2 pr-4 tabular-nums", BAND_TEXT[band])}>
                    {g.section.mean_coverage}%
                  </td>
                  <td className="py-2 pr-4 tabular-nums text-muted-foreground">
                    {/*
                      * A marker rather than a zero for a run graded before The
                      * Read was measured. A 0 would say she understood none of
                      * it, when the truth is that nobody asked.
                      */}
                    {g.section.read_matched_count === undefined
                      ? "not asked"
                      : `${g.section.read_matched_count} of ${g.section.stimuli_graded}`}
                  </td>
                  <td
                    className={cn(
                      "py-2 tabular-nums",
                      g.section.within_goal
                        ? "text-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald-strong))]"
                        : "text-muted-foreground",
                    )}
                  >
                    {minutes(g.section.total_seconds)}
                    <span className="text-muted-foreground"> / {g.section.goal_minutes}:00</span>
                  </td>
                </tr>
              );
            })}
          </tbody>
        </table>
      </div>

      {slipping.length > 0 && (
        <div>
          <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.16em] text-muted-foreground">
            What keeps slipping
          </p>
          <p className="mt-1 text-xs text-muted-foreground">
            How often each kind of detail goes missing, of the times it came up.
          </p>
          <ul className="mt-3 space-y-2">
            {slipping.map((t) => (
              <li key={t.kind} className="flex items-center gap-3 text-sm">
                <span className="w-52 shrink-0 text-foreground">{t.label}</span>
                <span className="h-1.5 flex-1 overflow-hidden rounded-full bg-[hsl(var(--muted))]">
                  <span
                    className="block h-full rounded-full bg-[hsl(24_85%_50%)]"
                    style={{ width: `${Math.round(t.share * 100)}%` }}
                  />
                </span>
                <span className="w-12 shrink-0 text-right tabular-nums text-muted-foreground">
                  {Math.round(t.share * 100)}%
                </span>
              </li>
            ))}
          </ul>
        </div>
      )}
    </div>
  );
}

/* The Read, Recall, Colour door.
 *
 * One evening, in two registers. The hero is the runner's own indigo lifted
 * into a dusk sky over a painted meadow, cut into depth planes that move at
 * their own rates as she scrolls; everything under it sits on cream paper in
 * the same light. The method is performed before it is explained, the four
 * steps are the next thing she meets, and the sections she can run sit
 * directly beneath.
 *
 * Every value is scoped to `.sl`. Nothing here touches the runner
 * (`recall/section.css`) or the shell outside this door. Class names beginning
 * `sl-` are the door's contract with its tests and its two wrappers; `rd-`
 * names are the design and may change freely.
 */

.sl {
  /* The hero panel's ink. Named so a rule that borrows it onto cream is
     visible as a mistake instead of reading as an ordinary colour. */
  --rd-on-dark: #fff;
  --rd-cream: #f6f3ea;
  --rd-paper: #fffdf8;
  --rd-paper-2: #f3efe4;
  --rd-ink: #16132b;
  --rd-ink-2: #46435f;
  --rd-ink-3: #6a6784;
  --rd-line: #e3ded2;
  --rd-line-2: #ede9df;
  --rd-indigo: #1a1635;
  --rd-indigo-2: #2a2450;
  --rd-lavender: #c9c3ec;
  --rd-apricot: #f3c9a0;
  --rd-apricot-light: #ffe8c8;
  --rd-marigold: #f2b544;
  --rd-marigold-ink: #6e4600;
  --rd-marigold-wash: rgba(242, 181, 68, 0.26);
  --rd-emerald: #0e6f4d;
  --rd-emerald-wash: rgba(14, 111, 77, 0.11);
  --rd-display: "Fraunces", "Iowan Old Style", "Times New Roman", serif;
  --rd-sans: "Inter", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, -apple-system, sans-serif;
  --rd-mono: "IBM Plex Mono", ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace;
  --rd-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);
  --rd-radius: 22px;
  --rd-radius-panel: 28px;

  min-height: 100%;
  background: var(--rd-cream);
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  font-family: var(--rd-sans);
  font-size: 16px;
  line-height: 1.5;
  -webkit-font-smoothing: antialiased;
  accent-color: var(--rd-ink);
  caret-color: var(--rd-marigold-ink);
  scrollbar-color: #c9c3b6 var(--rd-cream);
}
.sl ::selection {
  background: var(--rd-marigold-wash);
  color: var(--rd-ink);
}
.sl :focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid var(--rd-ink);
  outline-offset: 3px;
  box-shadow: none;
  border-radius: 6px;
}
.sl em {
  font-style: italic;
}

/* ---- the one micro-label style ---- */
.sl-label {
  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
  font-size: 11px;
  letter-spacing: 0.16em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
}

/* =============================================================== hero */

.rd-hero {
  padding: 14px 14px 0;
}
.rd-hero-panel {
  position: relative;
  isolation: isolate;
  overflow: hidden;
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  min-height: calc(100svh - 28px);
  border-radius: var(--rd-radius-panel);
  background: #2a2450;
  color: var(--rd-on-dark);
}

/* The scene: five planes and a stage, each its own layer. */
.mw {
  position: absolute;
  inset: 0;
  z-index: 0;
  overflow: hidden;
}
.mw-layer {
  position: absolute;
  inset: -6% 0 0 0;
  width: 100%;
  height: 106%;
  display: block;
  overflow: visible;
  will-change: transform;
}
.mw-sky {
  z-index: 1;
}
.mw-far {
  z-index: 2;
}
.mw-mid {
  z-index: 3;
}
.mw-near {
  z-index: 4;
}
.mw-grain {
  z-index: 5;
  inset: 0;
  height: 100%;
  opacity: 0.13;
  mix-blend-mode: soft-light;
  pointer-events: none;
}
.mw-stage {
  position: absolute;
  left: 0;
  right: 0;
  bottom: 11%;
  z-index: 6;
  display: flex;
  justify-content: center;
  padding: 0 22px;

.rc {
  /* ── The console: a deep plum field ─────────────────────────────────── */
  --rc-console: oklch(0.245 0.058 312);
  --rc-console-raised: oklch(0.315 0.062 312);
  --rc-console-hairline: oklch(0.385 0.055 312);
  --rc-console-ink: oklch(0.968 0.014 312);
  --rc-console-ink-dim: oklch(0.815 0.035 312);
  --rc-console-ink-faint: oklch(0.7 0.05 312);

  /* ── The page: a violet-cast white, never cream, never grey ─────────── */
  --rc-page: oklch(0.979 0.008 312);
  --rc-card: oklch(0.995 0.004 312);
  --rc-fill: oklch(0.958 0.012 312);
  --rc-ink: oklch(0.235 0.032 312);
  --rc-ink-muted: oklch(0.475 0.03 312);
  --rc-ink-faint: oklch(0.505 0.035 312);
  --rc-hairline: oklch(0.9 0.014 312);
  --rc-hairline-soft: oklch(0.945 0.011 312);

  /* ── The four categories. Each is a KIND of feedback, not a severity. ─
     `-ink` is the text weight, measured to clear 4.5:1 on both the page and
     its own soft field; `-soft` is that field; `-lit` is the console version,
     which has to clear the same bar against a dark plum instead. */
  --rc-detail-ink: oklch(0.475 0.135 12);
  --rc-detail-soft: oklch(0.952 0.028 12);
  --rc-detail-lit: oklch(0.775 0.115 14);

  --rc-read-ink: oklch(0.46 0.095 218);
  --rc-read-soft: oklch(0.948 0.03 218);
  --rc-read-lit: oklch(0.79 0.1 210);

  --rc-colour-ink: oklch(0.475 0.105 72);
  --rc-colour-soft: oklch(0.955 0.04 82);
  --rc-colour-lit: oklch(0.83 0.13 82);

  --rc-method-ink: oklch(0.455 0.09 278);
  --rc-method-soft: oklch(0.951 0.026 278);
  --rc-method-lit: oklch(0.79 0.085 280);

  /* Sound. Not a category, and the only thing on the page that moves. Held at
     328 rather than nearer the reds: the playhead lands on the same line as the
     dropped-Detail chips, and twenty degrees of hue is not enough separation
     between "this is playing" and "this is what you lost". */
  --rc-signal: oklch(0.545 0.225 328);
  --rc-signal-bright: oklch(0.715 0.2 328);
  --rc-signal-soft: oklch(0.957 0.032 328);
  --rc-signal-ink: oklch(0.995 0.005 328);

  /* The two faint inks are set where they are because that is where they clear
     4.5:1 on their own ground, measured in the browser rather than assumed.
     Every label on this card is 10px, so there is no large-text exemption to
     lean on. */
  --rc-display: "Fraunces", "Iowan Old Style", "Times New Roman", serif;
  --rc-body: "Lexend", "Outfit", ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif;
  --rc-mono: "IBM Plex Mono", ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace;

  /* Exponential ease. No bounce anywhere on this page: it is a page about
     being told what you got wrong, and a spring reads as a toy. */
  --rc-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);

  background: var(--rc-page);
  color: var(--rc-ink);
  font-family: var(--rc-body);
}

/* The card is read on a laptop in a long sitting, so the body column is capped
   at a measure rather than at the shell's container width. */
.rc__measure {
  margin-inline: auto;
  max-width: 72ch;
}

/* ── Micro-labels ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   One label style for the whole card. Every section of a stimulus wears it,
   which is what makes the order legible: she learns four labels once and then
   knows where she is on all twenty-two. */
.rc__label {
  color: var(--rc-ink-faint);
  font-family: var(--rc-mono);
  font-size: 10px;
  font-weight: 500;
  letter-spacing: 0.14em;
  text-transform: uppercase;
}

.rc__label--onconsole {
  color: var(--rc-console-ink-faint);
}

.rc__display {
  font-family: var(--rc-display);
  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 96;
  font-weight: 380;
  letter-spacing: -0.02em;
  line-height: 1.02;
}

/* ── The console ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */

.rc__console {
  background: var(--rc-console);
  color: var(--rc-console-ink);
}

.rc__console-divider {
  border-color: var(--rc-console-hairline);
}

/* ── The ledger of twenty-two ─────────────────────────────────────────────
   The whole section as one row of marks, so the shape of the run is visible
   before any one stimulus is opened. Twenty-two accordion rows are a list;
   this is a picture. */
.rc__ledger {
  display: grid;
  gap: 4px;
  grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(28px, 1fr));
}

.rc__tick {
  align-items: center;
