Tool result:
export function SectionReportCard({
  grade,
  onDone,
  onStartFocusSet,
  loadModelRuns = listModelRuns,
}: SectionReportCardProps) {
  const { section, stimuli } = grade;
  /*
   * One open at a time, and the first one open on arrival.
   *
   * The card used to open with twenty-two closed rows, which meant the default
   * state of the screen she waited twenty-five minutes for was a list of
   * headings. Something is always open now, and the ledger says which.
   */
  const [openStimulus, setOpenStimulus] = useState<number>(0);
  const clip = useClip();
  const modelRuns = useModelRuns(grade.source_section, loadModelRuns);
  /** Her own recording, when the upload survived. */
  const hers =
    section.audio_bucket && section.audio_path
      ? { bucket: section.audio_bucket, path: section.audio_path }
      : null;

  const overall = useMemo(() => {
    const perfect = stimuli.filter((s) => s.percent === 100).length;
    const atStandard = stimuli.filter((s) => s.band === "at-the-standard").length;
    /*
     * The run's own band. Computed from the mean the same way a stimulus's is
     * from its own percent, so the headline and the ledger cannot disagree
     * about what 86 means.
     */
    const band = BAND[bandFor(section.mean_coverage)];
    /*
     * Whether the marking of this run can be trusted, in her terms.
     *
     * Two measurements, neither of which is about her. `decoy_failures` counts
     * stimuli where the judge credited a Detail belonging to a different
     * stimulus, which means it credited words she never said. `ungraded_count`
     * counts stimuli the judge never answered for, which are otherwise counted
     * as missed -- scoring her for our outage.
     */
    const suspect = (section.decoy_failures ?? 0) > 0;
    const unmarked = section.ungraded_count ?? 0;
    /** Whether The Read was measured at all. Older grades predate it. */
    const readMeasured = section.read_given_count !== undefined;
    /**
     * New grades carry both the assignment's size and each row's authored
     * position. Older grades carry neither, so they keep their historical
     * array-order rendering rather than having a section shape invented for
     * them from a slug.
     */
    const hasStoredPositions = stimuli.every((s) => s.item_order !== undefined);
    const sectionSize = hasStoredPositions ? section.stimuli_in_section : undefined;
    const missingRows = sectionSize === undefined ? 0 : Math.max(0, sectionSize - stimuli.length);

    /*
     * How she ran the section, as against how she did on it.
     *
     * Every one of these was measured by the grader and some were shown
     * nowhere. They are collected rather than rendered inline so a run with
     * nothing to say about it shows nothing at all, instead of an empty heading.
     */
    const notes: string[] = [];
    if (section.reciting_count > 0)
      notes.push(`${section.reciting_count} said back close to word for word`);
    if (section.listing_count > 0)
      notes.push(`${section.listing_count} told as a list rather than an argument`);
    if (section.uncoloured_count > 0)
      notes.push(`${section.uncoloured_count} with no reaction of your own`);
    if (section.worst_dead_air_seconds >= section.dead_air_threshold_seconds)
      notes.push(`Longest freeze inside a recall: ${section.worst_dead_air_seconds}s`);
    if ((section.chunks_read_aloud ?? 0) > 0)
      notes.push(
        `You read ${section.chunks_read_aloud} of them out loud before recalling. That is not marked against you, and reading silently is the harder, better version.`,
      );

    return {
      perfect,
      atStandard,
      band,
      next: nextStep(section, stimuli),
      suspect,
      unmarked,
      readMeasured,
      notes,
      sectionSize,
      missingRows,
    };
  }, [stimuli, section]);

  const s = stimuli[Math.min(openStimulus, stimuli.length - 1)];
  const key = `s${openStimulus}`;
  const band = s ? (s.reciting ? RECITED : BAND[s.band]) : null;
  const clipWindow = s?.recall_window_ms ?? s?.window_ms;
  const model = s ? modelRuns[s.slug] : undefined;
  const pairs = model?.walkthrough ? walkthroughPairs(model.walkthrough) : [];
  // Paired only when the standard's run has exactly one recall per authored
  // line; anything else is shown whole, as before.
  const pairedByLine = !!s?.lines?.length && pairs.length === s.lines.length;
  const held = s ? whatHeld(s) : [];
  /*
   * The highlight follows her own voice only.
   *
   * The grader stores a start and an end for every line SHE spoke. It stores
   * nothing of the kind for the standard's recording, so a card that
   * highlighted through both would be inventing the timings for one of them.
   */
  const hersPlaying =
    clip.playing === key || (clip.playing?.startsWith(`${key}-l`) ?? false);
  const activeLine = lineAtPlayhead(s?.lines, clip.positionMs, hersPlaying);

  return (
    <div className="rc mx-auto max-w-[820px] overflow-hidden rounded-[20px] border border-[var(--rc-hairline)] shadow-[0_1px_2px_rgba(20,20,30,0.04),0_12px_32px_-12px_rgba(20,20,30,0.12)]">
      {/* ── The console ────────────────────────────────────────────────── */}
      <header className="rc__console px-6 py-7 md:px-9 md:py-9">
        <p className="rc__label rc__label--onconsole">
          {grade.attempt_no > 1 ? `Report Card · Attempt ${grade.attempt_no}` : "Report Card"}
        </p>

        {/* The band leads. The number is available and does not go first. */}
        <p className="rc__display mt-3 text-[40px] text-[var(--rc-console-ink)] md:text-[52px]">
          {overall.band.label}
        </p>
        <p className="mt-2.5 text-sm text-[var(--rc-console-ink-dim)]">
          {overall.atStandard} of {stimuli.length} at the standard
          {overall.perfect > 0 ? `, ${overall.perfect} with nothing dropped` : ""}.
        </p>

        {/*
          * What to do tomorrow, in one sentence or not at all.
          *
          * Every other line on this card describes what happened. This is the
          * only one that asks for something, which is why it is one line and
          * why a run with nothing clear to say gets none.
          */}
        {overall.next && (
          <p className="mt-5 max-w-[58ch] border-l-2 border-[var(--rc-signal)] pl-4 text-[15px] leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-console-ink)]">
            {overall.next}
          </p>
        )}

        {/*
          * The three figures.
          *
          * Coverage answers "did you hold it". The Read answers "did you
          * understand it", and it is the criterion the method is built around.
          * The clock is third because it is the one a student will read first
          * if you let her, and it is the least important of the three.
          */}
        <div className="rc__console-divider mt-7 grid gap-6 border-t pt-6 sm:grid-cols-3">
          <Figure
            label="Held"
            value={`${section.mean_coverage}%`}
            /* "across 22 of 22" is noise on a run where everything saved, and
               the one fact she needs on a run where it did not. */
            note={
              overall.missingRows > 0 && overall.sectionSize !== undefined
                ? `said back in your own words, across the ${section.stimuli_graded} of ${overall.sectionSize} that saved`
                : "of what was on the page, said back in your own words"
            }
          />
          {overall.readMeasured && (
            <Figure
              label="Understood"
              value={`${section.read_matched_count} of ${section.stimuli_graded}`}
              note={
                (section.no_read_count ?? 0) > 0
                  ? `where you found the move. On ${section.no_read_count} you did not give a Read at all.`
                  : "where you found the move the argument turns on"
              }
            />
          )}
          <div>
            <p className="rc__label rc__label--onconsole">Time</p>
            <p className="rc__display mt-1.5 text-[28px] text-[var(--rc-console-ink)]">
              {minutes(section.total_seconds)}
            </p>
            <p className="mt-1 text-xs leading-snug text-[var(--rc-console-ink-dim)]">
              against a {section.goal_minutes} minute goal
            </p>
            {/*
              * The breaks, said out loud.
              *
              * The figure above is her drill time and correctly has the pauses
              * taken out of it -- the clock stops with the recorder. Left at
              * that, a sitting broken by ten minutes at the door would read
              * exactly like one run straight through, and a student comparing
              * this week to last would be comparing two different things
              * without being told. It is not a rebuke: stepping away is allowed
              * and the alternative was throwing the section away.
              */}
            {(section.paused_seconds ?? 0) > 0 && (
              <p className="mt-1.5 text-xs leading-snug text-[var(--rc-console-ink-dim)]">
                {minutes(section.paused_seconds ?? 0)} paused across{" "}
                {section.pause_count === 1 ? "one break" : `${section.pause_count} breaks`}, which
                the time above does not include.
              </p>
            )}
            {/*
              * The goal counts only if every stimulus was recalled, coloured and
              * given a Read. Collapsing that into one boolean is what let a
              * student skip the colouring, finish early and be told she made the
              * goal, so the qualifier sits directly under the time.
              */}
            {section.within_time && !section.within_goal && (
              <p className="mt-1.5 text-xs leading-snug text-[var(--rc-console-ink-dim)]">
                Inside the time, but the goal only counts with every stimulus coloured and given a
                Read.
              </p>
            )}
            {/*
              * Over the goal, which used to get nothing at all. Not a rebuke.
              * Early runs are slow by design and the briefing already says to go
              * slower while it is new, so this says what the time is for rather
              * than that she was late.
              */}
            {section.within_time === false && (
              <p className="mt-1.5 text-xs leading-snug text-[var(--rc-console-ink-dim)]">
                Over the goal, which early runs are meant to be. The goal is what the drill costs
                once it is a habit, not a limit on today.
              </p>
            )}
          </div>
        </div>

        {/*
          * How she ran it, kept off the figures.
          *
          * These are observations about method, not about score, and mixing them
          * into the numbers is what made them read as five more things she got
          * wrong.
          */}
        {overall.notes.length > 0 && (
          <div className="rc__console-divider mt-6 border-t pt-6">
            <p className="rc__label rc__label--onconsole">Worth working on</p>
            <ul className="mt-3 space-y-1.5">
              {overall.notes.map((note) => (
                <li key={note} className="max-w-[64ch] text-sm leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-console-ink-dim)]">
                  {note}
                </li>
              ))}
            </ul>
          </div>
        )}

        {/*
          * When the marking itself is in doubt, she is told before the number.
          *
          * All three of these are about us. A stimulus the judge never answered
          * for is counted as missed, which scores her for our outage, and she is
          * the only person in a position to notice the score looks wrong.
          */}
        {(overall.suspect || overall.unmarked > 0 || overall.missingRows > 0) && (
          <div className="rc__console-divider mt-6 space-y-2 border-t pt-6 text-sm leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-console-ink-dim)]">
            {overall.missingRows > 0 && overall.sectionSize !== undefined && (
              <p>
                {overall.missingRows} of the {overall.sectionSize} did not save, so they are not
                marked here. That is ours, not yours.
              </p>
            )}
            {overall.unmarked > 0 && (
              <p>
                {overall.unmarked} {overall.unmarked === 1 ? "stimulus" : "stimuli"} could not be
                marked, so {overall.unmarked === 1 ? "it counts" : "they count"} as nothing carried.
                That is ours, not yours. Come back later and it will have been marked properly.
              </p>
            )}
            {overall.suspect && (
              <p>
                Our own check on this marking came back unhappy, so treat the number on one or two
                of these lightly. Joshua can see the same thing.
              </p>
            )}
          </div>
        )}

        {/* ── The ledger ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
            The shape of the run in one glance, and the only navigation. */}
        <div className="rc__console-divider mt-7 border-t pt-6">
          <p className="rc__label rc__label--onconsole">The run</p>
          <div className="rc__ledger mt-3.5">
            {stimuli.map((st, i) => {
              const m = st.ungraded ? "ungraded" : st.reciting ? "recited" : BAND[st.band].mark;
              return (
                <button
                  key={st.slug}
                  type="button"
                  className="rc__tick"
                  aria-current={i === openStimulus}
                  onClick={() => setOpenStimulus(i)}
                >
                  {/* The accessible name. The digit below is decoration for a
                      reader who can see the ledger. */}
                  <span className="sr-only">Stimulus {st.item_order ?? i + 1}</span>
                  <span className={cn("rc__mark", `rc__mark--${m}`)} aria-hidden />
                  <span className="rc__tick-n" aria-hidden>
                    {st.item_order ?? i + 1}
                  </span>
                </button>
              );
            })}
          </div>
        </div>
      </header>

      {/* ── The open stimulus ──────────────────────────────────────────── */}
      {s && band && (
        <div className="px-6 py-7 md:px-9 md:py-9">
          <div className="rc__measure">
            <div className="flex flex-wrap items-baseline justify-between gap-x-6 gap-y-2">
              <div>
                <p className="rc__label">
                  Stimulus {s.item_order ?? openStimulus + 1} of {stimuli.length}
                </p>
                <p className="rc__display mt-1.5 text-[26px] text-[var(--rc-ink)]">{band.label}</p>
              </div>
              <p className="text-sm text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]">{howMuch(s.percent)}</p>
            </div>

            {/*
              * Hers, then his, as one control.
              *
              * The pair is the lesson. One recording is a correction and two is
              * a demonstration, which is the whole argument for putting audio on
              * this page at all. They were two scattered text links at the
              * bottom of an expanded row; here they are the transport, at the
              * top, where a thing you press lives.
              */}
            {(hers || model?.audio) && (
              <div className="mt-5 flex flex-wrap items-center gap-3">
                <div className="rc__transport">
                  {hers && clipWindow && (
                    <button
                      type="button"
                      className="rc__source"
                      aria-pressed={clip.playing === key}
                      onClick={() => void clip.play(key, hers, clipWindow.start, clipWindow.end)}
                    >
                      {clip.playing === key ? "Stop" : "Hear it back"}
                    </button>
                  )}
                  {model?.audio && (
                    <button
                      type="button"
                      className="rc__source"
                      aria-pressed={clip.playing === `${key}-model`}

## assistant

Tool call: Bash
{"command": "cd src/features/activity-centre/components && sed -n '720,1000p' SectionReportCard.tsx | grep -v '^\\s*//' ", "description": "Read Report Card stimulus body render"}

## user

Tool result:
                      aria-pressed={clip.playing === `${key}-model`}
                      onClick={() => void clip.play(`${key}-model`, model.audio!)}
                    >
                      {clip.playing === `${key}-model` ? "Stop" : "Hear it done right"}
                    </button>
                  )}
                </div>
                {hersPlaying && !!s.lines?.length && (
                  <p className="text-xs text-[var(--rc-ink-faint)]" role="status">
                    Following your recording, line by line.
                  </p>
                )}
              </div>
            )}

            <div className="mt-7 space-y-6">
              {/*
                * The passage, so she can reread what she was holding without
                * leaving the card. Folded, because on the second read of the
                * page she wants her own words, not the source.
                */}
              {s.stimulus_text && (
                <details className="rounded-lg border border-[var(--rc-hairline)] bg-[var(--rc-card)] px-4 py-3">
                  <summary className="rc__label cursor-pointer">The passage</summary>
                  <p className="mt-3 text-[15px] leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink)]">
                    {s.stimulus_text}
                  </p>
                </details>
              )}

              {s.ungraded ? (
                <Movement label="What happened" tone="method">
                  <p className="text-[15px] leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]">
                    This one could not be marked, so it is showing as nothing carried. That is ours,
                    not yours.
                  </p>
                </Movement>
              ) : (
                <>
                  {/* Specific congratulation, ahead of the correction. */}
                  {held.length > 0 && (
                    <Movement label="What you held" tone="colour">
                      <ul className="space-y-1.5">
                        {held.map((h) => (
                          <li key={h} className="text-[15px] leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink)]">
                            {h}
                          </li>
                        ))}
                      </ul>
                    </Movement>
                  )}

                  {/*
                    * The three that mattered, ahead of the whole list.
                    *
                    * Every language app that does spoken feedback well surfaces
                    * a small actionable set rather than every imperfection
                    * (Busuu names three), and the grader has been returning
                    * exactly that in `bigMisses` since the day it was written.
                    * It was rendered as one grey line at the bottom of the
                    * line-by-line block, under everything, which is the same as
                    * not rendering it. A student who reads one thing here
                    * should read these.
                    */}
                  <Movement label="What slipped" tone="detail">
                    {s.missed.length === 0 ? (
                      <p className="text-[15px] leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]">
                        {s.reciting
                          ? "It is all in there, which is what saying it back word for word does rather than what you did."
                          : "Nothing slipped on this one. You kept the whole thing."}
                      </p>
                    ) : s.bigMisses?.length ? (
                      <>
                        <ul className="space-y-1.5">
                          {s.bigMisses.map((m) => (
                            <li key={m} className="text-[15px] leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink)]">
                              {m}
                            </li>
                          ))}
                        </ul>
                        {/* The rest, for a student who wants all of it. Folded,
                            because the three above are the ones to act on. */}
                        {s.missed.length > 0 && (
                          <details className="mt-3">
                            <summary className="rc__label cursor-pointer">
                              Everything else that slipped ({s.missed.length})
                            </summary>
                            <ul className="mt-2 space-y-1.5">
                              {s.missed.map((m) => (
                                <li
                                  key={m}
                                  className="text-sm leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]"
                                >
                                  {m}
                                </li>
                              ))}
                            </ul>
                          </details>
                        )}
                      </>
                    ) : (
                      <ul className="space-y-1.5">
                        {s.missed.map((m) => (
                          <li key={m} className="text-[15px] leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink)]">
                            {m}
                          </li>
                        ))}
                      </ul>
                    )}

                    {/* How she ran this one, under what she lost on it. */}
                    {(s.reciting ||
                      s.listing ||
                      !s.coloured ||
                      (s.coloured && !s.colourSpecific) ||
                      s.dead_air_seconds >= section.dead_air_threshold_seconds) && (
                      <div className="mt-3 space-y-2">
                        {s.reciting && (
                          <p className="text-sm leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]">
                            This one came back close to word for word, so the details on it were
                            carried by the page rather than by you. Say it in your own words instead,
                            even clumsily. That is the version that tells you what you actually have.
                          </p>
                        )}
                        {s.listing && (
                          <p className="text-sm leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]">
                            This came out as a run of facts. Tell it as one connected argument.
                          </p>
                        )}
                        {!s.coloured && (
                          <p className="text-sm leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]">
                            Nothing of your own on this one. React to it, even briefly.
                          </p>
                        )}
                        {/*
                          * Measured on 18 of 22 in the trial and shown on none.
                          * It is the one standard the whole method turns on: a
                          * reaction that would fit any other stimulus is not a
                          * reaction to this one.
                          */}
                        {s.coloured && !s.colourSpecific && (
                          <p className="text-sm leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]">
                            Your reaction would have fitted any stimulus. React to what this one
                            actually claims.
                          </p>
                        )}
                        {s.dead_air_seconds >= section.dead_air_threshold_seconds && (
                          <p className="text-sm leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]">
                            You went quiet for {s.dead_air_seconds}s in the middle of this recall.
                            That is the moment it went, and it is worth finding out what was there.
                          </p>
                        )}
                      </div>
                    )}
                  </Movement>

                  {/* Her Read, which is the half that predicts a score. */}
                  {s.readGiven !== undefined && (
                    <Movement label="Your Read" tone="read">
                      <p className="text-[15px] leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink)]">
                        {!s.readGiven
                          ? "You stopped at the retelling. Say what the argument is doing, not only what it says."
                          : s.readMatches
                            ? "You found the move."
                            : "You gave a Read, but not the one this argument turns on."}
                      </p>
                      {/*
                        * Why, in the judge's words, and what the key names. Both
                        * were stored and neither was shown, so "not the one this
                        * argument turns on" was a verdict with no reason and no
                        * answer. The reason is what she can dispute; the key is
                        * what she can learn from.
                        */}
                      {s.readReason && (
                        <p className="mt-1.5 text-sm leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]">
                          {s.readReason}
                        </p>
                      )}
                      {s.the_read && (
                        <p className="rc__panel mt-3 text-[15px] italic leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink)]">
                          The Read this one turns on: {s.the_read}
                        </p>
                      )}
                    </Movement>
                  )}

                  {/*
                    * Line by line, because that is how she worked.
                    *
                    * She reads one line silently, looks away and says it back.
                    * The cut is worked out after the run from the silence in
                    * front of each spoken line, so it is approximate -- which is
                    * why it says what she carried and dropped rather than
                    * scoring the line. Coverage above is unchanged.
                    *
                    * Three things stacked per line, always in this order: the
                    * line as written, what she said for it, what the standard
                    * says for it. That is the comparison the drill is about, and
                    * pressing play walks the highlight down it.
                    */}
                  {!!s.lines?.length && (
                    <Movement label="Line by line" tone="detail">
                      <ol>
                        {s.lines.map((line) => {
                          const lineKey = `${key}-l${line.n}`;
                          const playable =
                            hers && line.startMs !== undefined && line.endMs !== undefined;
                          return (
                            <li
                              key={line.n}
                              className="rc__line"
                              data-playing={activeLine === line.n}
                            >
                              <div className="flex items-start justify-between gap-4">
                                <p className="rc__line-text">
                                  {line.n}. {line.text}
                                </p>
                                {playable && (
                                  <button
                                    type="button"
                                    className="rc__hear shrink-0"
                                    data-playing={clip.playing === lineKey}
                                    onClick={() =>
                                      void clip.play(lineKey, hers!, line.startMs, line.endMs)
                                    }
                                  >
                                    {clip.playing === lineKey ? "Stop" : "Hear it"}
                                  </button>
                                )}
                              </div>

                              <p className="rc__line-spoken mt-2">
                                {line.spoken || "You did not say anything for this line."}
                              </p>

                              {line.note && (
                                <p className="mt-2 text-sm leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink)]">
                                  {line.note}
                                </p>
                              )}

                              {(line.carried.length > 0 || line.dropped.length > 0) && (
                                <div className="mt-2.5 space-y-1.5">
                                  {line.carried.length > 0 && (
                                    <div className="rc__chips">
                                      <span className="rc__label mt-1 mr-1">Held</span>
                                      {line.carried.map((c) => (
                                        <span key={c} className="rc__chip rc__chip--held">
                                          {c}
                                        </span>
                                      ))}
                                    </div>
                                  )}
                                  {line.dropped.length > 0 && (
                                    <div className="rc__chips">
                                      <span className="rc__label mt-1 mr-1">Dropped</span>
                                      {line.dropped.map((d) => (
                                        <span key={d} className="rc__chip rc__chip--dropped">
                                          {d}
                                        </span>
                                      ))}
                                    </div>
                                  )}
                                </div>
                              )}

                              {pairedByLine && pairs[line.n - 1]?.recall && (
                                <div className="mt-3 rounded-lg border border-[var(--rc-hairline-soft)] bg-[var(--rc-card)] px-3.5 py-3">
                                  {/* A span, not a paragraph: the whole-run
                                      walkthrough below owns the paragraph form
                                      of this label, and only one of the two is
                                      ever on screen. */}
                                  <span className="rc__label block">Done right</span>
                                  <p className="mt-1.5 text-sm leading-relaxed text-[var(--rc-ink-muted)]">
                                    {pairs[line.n - 1].recall}
                                  </p>
                                </div>
                              )}
                            </li>
                          );
                        })}

  }, [countdown, done, paused, seconds]);

  /*
   * Hold the app still for the length of the run.
   *
   * Two things in the browser will end a section without asking. The app's own
   * freshness check reloads the tab when a newer bundle has been published, on
   * every return to it -- so publishing mid-section and her glancing away was
   * enough to lose twenty-five minutes. And a reflex Cmd-R or a closed tab does
   * the same thing by hand.
   *
   * The first is suppressed outright: the reload is deferred until the run
   * ends, which costs her one stale bundle for one section. The second cannot
   * be suppressed, so it is questioned -- `beforeunload` is the browser's own
   * prompt and the only guard available, and it is deliberately registered for
   * the whole run rather than only once she has spoken, because the expensive
   * thing to lose is the recording and that starts here.
   */
  useEffect(() => {
    const release = beginLiveRun();
    const warn = (e: BeforeUnloadEvent) => {
      e.preventDefault();
      // Set for the browsers that still require it; none of them show the
      // string any more, and the prompt is generic by design.
      e.returnValue = "";
    };
    window.addEventListener("beforeunload", warn);
    return () => {
      window.removeEventListener("beforeunload", warn);
      release();
    };
  }, []);

  /*
   * Press record at the very beginning. Before the first chunk, not on the
   * first keypress: the drill's first act is reading, and a recording that
   * starts on the first Space has already missed it.
   */
  useEffect(() => {
    void startRecording();
    // Mount only. Restarting a recorder mid-section is the one thing the drill
    // forbids outright.
    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps
  }, []);

  /*
   * A run she leaves has no backup worth keeping.
   *
   * Leaving throws the whole run away (ADR 0023), and the recorder closes with
   * the hook. What the backup store still holds for this run id is the pieces
   * of a sitting that will never be graded: recovery would take them for a
   * crashed run and upload them, and on 2026-09-08 it did exactly that, over
   * the finished recording of the same assignment. So they go now.
   *
   * A run that ENDED is a different case and is left alone: its own finish
   * path discards the backup once the upload lands, and if she leaves the
   * Report Card handover mid-upload the pieces are the only copy there is.
   * Ordering matters here: React runs cleanups in the order the effects were
   * declared, and the one that cancels retries has already run by now.
   */
  useEffect(
    () => () => {
      // `saving`, not `finishing`: the unmount cleanup above raises `finishing`
      // for every unmount, ended or abandoned, while `saving` is set by the
      // finish path alone.
      if (saving.current) return;
      void discard(runId.current);
    },
    [],
  );

  /**
   * One key press, twice per stimulus. This is the whole interaction: no mouse
   * in the loop, and no other signal either -- advancing on detected silence
   * was considered and rejected, because a timeout would cut her off
   * mid-sentence if she paused to think (ADR 0013).
....
   * Leaving, in two steps.
   *
   * Before the section starts there is nothing to lose and the button is just a
   * way out. Once she is working, one click on a permanently visible control
   * would throw away up to twenty-five minutes that is deliberately never
   * scored in part, so it asks first.
   */
  const askToExit = useCallback(() => {
    if (countdown !== null) onExit?.();
    else setConfirmingExit(true);
  }, [countdown, onExit]);

  /*
   * A run that ENDS while paused still has to close its books.
   *
   * `done` is reachable from a pause -- she can be paused on the last stimulus
   * when a resume fails, or the last press can be followed by one. The break
   * that is still open at that moment has never been added to the total, so
   * without this the final row would report a paused-time of everything except
   * the pause she was actually in.
   */
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!done || pausedSince.current === null) return;
    pausedTotalMs.current += Math.max(0, now() - pausedSince.current);
    pausedSince.current = null;
  }, [done, now]);

   * The end of the run: stop the recorder, upload, and re-send everything.

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!done || saving.current) return;
    saving.current = true;
    finishing.current = true;
    for (const timer of retryTimers.current) clearTimeout(timer);
    retryTimers.current.clear();
    let cancelled = false;
    void (async () => {
      const total = seconds(startedAt.current);

      /*
       * Stop the recorder and put the file away.

       * A failed upload does not fail the section. Twenty minutes of work and
       * the timings are still worth keeping, and the grader treats a file that

      const finished = await stopRecording();
      const path = audioPath();

         * nothing anywhere saying why. It is a real fault and it is hers to
         * know about, because the recovery needs her to open the section again.
         */
        let upload = (
          await supabase.storage
            .from(RECORDING_BUCKET)
            .upload(path, finished.blob, { contentType: finished.mimeType, upsert: true })
        ).error;
        if (upload) {

      /*
       * A refused microphone is the loud fault and wins the line. A live one

      recording={recording && !micRefused && !paused}

      onPause={countdown === null ? pauseRun : undefined}

1859:.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-essay h3 {
.sl-briefing-page {
  max-width: 880px;
  margin: 0 auto;
  padding: 40px 20px 96px;
  display: grid;
  gap: 32px;
}
/* Stacked on purpose. It was space-between, and at this page's width the
   title block and the CTA never fit on one line, so the CTA always wrapped
   under the tagline: the layout that shipped was accidental. */
.sl-bp-head {
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  gap: 22px;
  align-items: flex-start;
}
/* The notice, un-boxed. It shared fill, hairline and radius with the four step
   cards below it, so the one sentence written to lower her shoulders read as a
   fifth thing to do. A plain line in ink, the page speaking. */
.sl-briefing-page .sl-notice {
  padding: 0;
  background: none;
  border: 0;
  border-radius: 0;
  font-size: 17px;
  line-height: 1.5;
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  max-width: 62ch;
}
/* A landing-page pill on a task page. The lift and outer shadow are the
   door's persuade register; on paper the fill darkening is enough. */
.sl-briefing-page .sl-start,
.sl-briefing-page .sl-start:hover,
.sl-briefing-page .sl-start:active {
  transform: none;
  box-shadow: none;
}
/* The hero classes are written for the door's dark purple ground: .sl-title is
   white at up to 96px, .sl-tagline is white with a drop shadow. This page sits
   on paper, so both were being drawn in white on cream. The briefing page uses
   the same classes for the same copy, so it restates the colour and the size
   here rather than forking them. */
/* The focus ring, per control rather than once at the top.
   `.sl :focus-visible` sets a ring for the whole world, and loses the cascade on
   the two button classes, so Start and Back matched :focus-visible and computed
   outline-style: none. A keyboard student could not see where she was on the one
   control that begins the run, and Back had no indicator at all.

   The colour depends on the ground, and these buttons appear on both: the door
   draws them on the dark hero panel, every other surface on cream. */
.sl .sl-start:focus-visible,
.sl .sl-brief:focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid var(--rd-ink);
  outline-offset: 3px;
}
.rd-hero .sl-start:focus-visible,
.rd-hero .sl-brief:focus-visible {
  outline-color: var(--rd-cream);
}
/* 43x44 was under the 44x44 floor, and this is the exit control. */
.sl-brief {
  min-width: 44px;
}

/* Two more hero classes drawn in white for the door's dark panel. On paper they
   were invisible: the drill's own name, and a Back button nobody could see. */
.sl-briefing-page .sl-chip {
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  background: var(--rd-paper);
  border-color: var(--rd-line);
  backdrop-filter: none;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-brief {
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
  text-decoration-color: var(--rd-line);
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-brief:hover {
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  text-decoration-color: var(--rd-ink-2);
}
/* A heading inside the long version was set larger than the section heading
   above it, so the page read as if the essay outranked its own label. */
.sl-briefing-page .sl-bp-essay h3 {
  font-size: 20px;
  margin-top: 28px;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-title {
  color: var(--rd-ink);
  font-size: clamp(30px, 3.4vw, 46px);
  line-height: 1.06;
  letter-spacing: -0.02em;
  margin: 14px 0 0;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-title em {
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
}
/* .sl-tagline is white with a drop shadow, which is right on the door's dark
   hero and unreadable here: this page sits on paper. The briefing page reuses
   the class for the same line of copy, so it restates the colour rather than
   forking the class. */
.sl-briefing-page .sl-tagline {
  margin: 12px 0 0;
  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
  text-shadow: none;
}
.sl-briefing-page .sl-tagline strong {
  color: var(--rd-ink);
}
/* The long version, closed by default. */
.sl-bp-more > summary {
  cursor: pointer;
  list-style: none;
  padding: 14px 0;
  border-top: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
}
/* The summary's heading used to inherit body weight, so the only help on the
   page looked like a stray sentence beside an uppercase h2. Same treatment as
   its sibling, and a drawn chevron instead of a " +" that some readers spoke. */
.sl-bp-more > summary {
  display: flex;

## user

Tool result:
/* Read, Recall, Colour, section mode.
 *
 * The v1 runner sits on a dark field because it shows one chunk at a time and
 * nothing during recall: there is barely anything on screen to light. A section
 * is the opposite surface. The whole stimulus is present for the whole
 * stimulus, it is the only thing on screen, and she is reading it closely for
 * twenty minutes. That is a document, and a document belongs on paper.
 *
 * So this screen wears the real section's clothes. `OfficialDrillFrame` already
 * mirrors LawHub -- indigo field, white header rail, one centred white card,
 * Fraunces at 19px -- and that is the surface she will sit on test day. Every
 * value below is taken from it rather than invented, and the answering half is
 * removed rather than restyled. No new dialect: this is the drill room.
 *
 * Everything after the frame exists to keep the text in exactly the same place
 * while its state changes.
 */

.ac-section {
  --sec-field: #1a1635;
  --sec-paper: #ffffff;
  --sec-ink: #111827;
  --sec-rule: #cbd5e1;
  --sec-chrome: #334155;
  --sec-warn: #b45309;
  --sec-sans: "Helvetica Neue", Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif;

  display: flex;
  min-height: 100vh;
  flex-direction: column;
  background: var(--sec-field);
  font-family: var(--sec-sans);
}

/* ---- the white rail ---------------------------------------------------- */

/* One column per child, and the count has to be kept: declared short once, the
   exit wrapped onto a second row and the rail grew the header from 46px to 96px
   on the drill's only screen. Six now -- position, rail, clock, the recording
   mark, Pause, the way out. */
.ac-section-head {
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: auto 1fr auto auto auto auto;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 1.25rem;
  padding: 0.75rem 1.5rem;
  border-bottom: 1px solid #e2e8f0;
  background: var(--sec-paper);
  color: var(--sec-chrome);
  font-size: 0.8125rem;
}

/* Where she is in the section. The clock sits beside it; see
   `.ac-section-clock` for why it counts up rather than down. */
.ac-section-where {
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
}

.ac-section-where b {
  font-weight: 600;
...
/* ---- the paper --------------------------------------------------------- */

.ac-section-field {
  display: flex;
  flex: 1;
  padding: 1rem;
}

/* The floor is a floor, not a promise the viewport can keep.
 *
 * `min-height: 700px` was written for a desktop and is taller than a 13-inch
 * laptop has room for. At a 664px viewport the card ran to 778px and pushed the
 * footer -- the instruction, the presence bar and the microphone error, which
 * are the only words on the screen telling her what to do and whether she is
 * being heard -- 55px below the fold. She would never see "Nothing is reaching
 * the microphone" on that machine.
 *
 * The `max-width: 640px` rule below did zero the floor, but it is a WIDTH
 * query, and the laptop that fails this is 1440 wide. Height is what was short,
 * so height is what the cap is written against.
 *
 * `min()` rather than a smaller constant, so the same bug cannot return at
 * 600px: the card asks for 700 and takes whatever the viewport can actually
 * spare, less the header and the field's own padding. On anything taller than
 * about 800px nothing changes at all. */
.ac-section-paper {
  display: flex;
  width: 100%;
  max-width: 1400px;
  min-height: min(700px, calc(100vh - 7rem));
  margin: 0 auto;
  flex-direction: column;
  overflow: hidden;
  border: 1px solid #cbd5e1;
  border-radius: 0.375rem;
  background: var(--sec-paper);
  box-shadow: 0 4px 18px rgb(0 0 0 / 0.35);
}

/* The stimulus is the only thing in the card, so its column is centred
   horizontally -- but it hangs from the top, exactly as it does in the column it
   used to share with a question.
 *
 * Centring it vertically was the first cut and it was wrong: stimuli run from
 * two lines to nine, so a centred block starts at a different height on every
 * one of the 23 and the whole page appears to jump between them. The spec bans
 * reflow inside a stimulus; this is the same failure one level up. */
.ac-section-body {
  display: flex;
  flex: 1;
  /* A flex item will not shrink below its content unless it is told it may. The
...
/* ---- leaving the drill room -------------------------------------------- */

/* The one composed moment in the drill.
 *
 * The runner is a cold indigo mirror of the real section; the Report Card is
 * warm paper. The jump between them was abrupt because there was nothing
 * between them: the field cut to the card in one frame, at the end of
 * twenty-five unbroken minutes, which is the moment she is most likely to
 * remember and the moment that felt most like a page reload.
 *
 * So the field lifts rather than cuts. The paper card holds its position and
 * fades with it, the indigo warms to the ground the Report Card sits on, and
 * the handover happens underneath. Nothing waits on it: the save, the upload
 * and the grading request all run through these nine hundred milliseconds, and
 * the transition is over long before any of them are.
 */
.ac-section.is-finishing {
  /* The Activity Centre's own canvas, by value rather than by eye.
     `--background` is scoped to `.activity-centre`, which this element is not
     inside, and landing two shades off it turns a handover into a visible step
     at the exact moment it is meant to be invisible. Kept beside the token it
     copies so a change to one is a change to look for in the other. */
  background: hsl(38 36% 93%);
  transition: background 900ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);
}

.ac-section.is-finishing .ac-section-head {
  opacity: 0;
  transition: opacity 500ms ease;
}

.ac-section.is-finishing .ac-section-paper {
  border-color: transparent;
  box-shadow: 0 1px 3px rgb(0 0 0 / 0.04);
  transition:
    box-shadow 900ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1),
    border-color 900ms ease;
}

/* What she reads while it happens. It arrives after the field has started to
   warm, so the sentence appears on paper rather than on the drill room. */
.ac-section-finished {
  margin: 0;
  animation: ac-section-finished-in 700ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1) 200ms both;
  color: var(--sec-ink);
  font-family: Fraunces, ui-serif, Georgia, serif;
  font-size: 22px;
  font-optical-sizing: none;
  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 14;
  text-align: center;
}

.ac-section-finished span {
  display: block;
  margin-top: 0.75rem;
  color: #64748b;
  font-family: var(--sec-sans);
  font-size: 0.875rem;
  line-height: 1.6;
}

@keyframes ac-section-finished-in {
  from {
    opacity: 0;
    transform: translateY(6px);
  }
  to {
    opacity: 1;
    transform: none;
  }
}

@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  .ac-section.is-finishing,
  .ac-section.is-finishing .ac-section-head,
  .ac-section.is-finishing .ac-section-paper {
    transition: none;
  }

  .ac-section-finished {
    animation: none;
  }
}


/* The other half of the handover, on the surface that arrives.
 *
 * Applied by the Report Card's own container so the card rises onto the ground
 * the drill room just warmed to, instead of appearing on it. Slower than a
 * normal entrance and with no scale: this is the end of twenty-five minutes of
 * concentration, not a notification.
 */
.ac-section-handover {
  animation: ac-section-handover-in 800ms cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1) both;
}

@keyframes ac-section-handover-in {
  from {
    opacity: 0;
    transform: translateY(10px);
  }
  to {
    opacity: 1;
    transform: none;
  }
}

@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  .ac-section-handover {
    animation: none;
  }
